=\frac{1}{4\pi\mathbf{i}}\oint_{{\omega_{-}}\cup\omega^{\prime}_{-}}\frac{f_{\mathsf{t}}(w)}{f_{\mathsf{t}}(w)-1}\cdot\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}(w)}{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(w)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{i}))}\cdot\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}(w)}{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(w)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{j}))}{\rm d}w.

(274)

In the last integral we change variables by introducing
W={\mathsf{q}}^{w}
,
Z_{i}={\mathsf{q}}^{z({\mathsf{t}},u_{i})}
,
Z_{j}={\mathsf{q}}^{z({\mathsf{t}},u_{j})}
, and
F_{\mathsf{t}}(W)=f_{\mathsf{t}}(w)
. We get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\pi\mathbf{i}}\oint_{{\mathsf{q}}^{{\omega_{-}}}\cup{\mathsf{q}}^{\omega^{\prime}_{-}}}\frac{F_{\mathsf{t}}(W)}{F_{\mathsf{t}}(W)-1}\frac{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}W-W^{-1}}{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}W+W^{-1}-\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{i}-Z_{i}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}W-W^{-1}}{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}W+W^{-1}-\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{j}-Z_{j}^{-1}}\cdot\frac{{\rm d}W}{W}.

We compute this contour integral as (minus) the sum of the residues outside the integration contour:

•

As
W\to\infty
,
F_{\mathsf{t}}(W)\to{\mathsf{q}}^{{\mathsf{t}}-{\mathsf{T}}}
, as we computed right before (
230
). Hence, the integrand behaves as

\frac{{\mathsf{q}}^{{\mathsf{t}}-{\mathsf{T}}}}{{\mathsf{q}}^{{\mathsf{t}}-{\mathsf{T}}}-1}\cdot\frac{1}{W}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{W^{2}}\right),

and the residue is a constant which does not depend on
u_{i}
or
u_{j}
. Therefore, it vanishes in differentiations in (
273
).

•

By the symmetries (
6.2
) we also have a constant residue at
W\to 0
, which vanishes in differentiations.

•

As we have seen in Step 1 of the proof of Proposition
5.12
, outside our integration contour
F_{\mathsf{t}}(W)=1
is possible only at
W=\pm{\mathsf{q}}^{{\mathsf{t}}/2}\kappa^{-1}
. At these points the numerators
\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}W-W^{-1}
vanish and there are no residues.

•

At
W=Z_{i}
, the residue is

\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{F_{\mathsf{t}}(Z_{i})}{1-F_{\mathsf{t}}(Z_{i})}\cdot\frac{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{i}-Z_{i}^{-1}}{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{i}+Z_{i}^{-1}-\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{j}-Z_{j}^{-1}}.

•

At
W=\kappa^{-2}Z_{i}^{-1}{\mathsf{q}}^{{\mathsf{t}}}
the residue is the same.

•

At
W=Z_{j}
and at
W=\kappa^{-2}Z_{j}^{-1}{\mathsf{q}}^{{\mathsf{t}}}
the residues are

\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{F_{\mathsf{t}}(Z_{j})}{1-F_{\mathsf{t}}(Z_{j})}\cdot\frac{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{j}-Z_{j}^{-1}}{\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{j}+Z_{j}^{-1}-\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-{\mathsf{t}}}Z_{i}-Z_{i}^{-1}}.

Summing the four non-trivial residues, and setting
\tau=\min({\mathsf{t}}_{i},{\mathsf{t}}_{j})
, (
273
) evaluates to

-\partial_{u_{1}}\partial_{u_{2}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{\frac{f_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{i}))}{f_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{i}))-1}{\mathsf{b}}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{i}))-\frac{f_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{j}))}{f_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{j}))-1}{\mathsf{b}}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{j}))}{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{i}))-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{j}))}{\rm d}{\mathsf{t}}\\
=-\partial_{u_{1}}\partial_{u_{2}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{\mathsf{t}}\ln\bigl[{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{i}))-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},u_{j}))\bigr]{\rm d}{\mathsf{t}}=\ln\left[\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z(0,u_{i}))-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z(0,u_{j}))}{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z(\tau,u_{i}))-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z(\tau,u_{j}))}\right].

(275)

Recalling that this was the computation of the asymptotics of the second line in (
272
), we arrive at the covariance of (
269
).
∎

Next, we upgrade from convergence in distribution to convergence of moments.

Lemma 6.7
.

The convergence in Lemma
6.6
is also in the sense of moments.

Convergence in distribution together with uniform integrability implies the convergence of the moments. Hence, we need to show that the sums in (
271
) have moments bounded uniformly in
\varepsilon
. Thus, we would like to estimate the
2p
–th moment of the sum in (
271
) for each
p=1,2,\dots
. The Burkholder–Rosenthal inequality
[
95
, Equation (2.9)]
(see also
[
32
,
65
]
) gives that for each
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}_{p}>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)\right|^{2p}

(276)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}_{p}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2}\big|{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\bigg)^{p}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+{\mathfrak{C}}_{p}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2p}\big].

For the
m
-th term in the first sum on the righthand side of (
276
), denoting
{\mathsf{s}}=\varepsilon m
, thanks to Proposition
5.14
(by taking
z_{1}=z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u),z_{2}=\overline{z}({\mathsf{s}},u)
in (
207
)), we have

\left|\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2}\big|{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\right|\leqslant\varepsilon|\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2}\\
\times\left|\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\oint_{{\omega_{-}}}\frac{\tilde{f}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)}{\tilde{f}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)-1}\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(w){\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u))}{({\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u)))^{2}}\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(w){\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(\overline{z}({\mathsf{s}},u))}{({\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(\overline{z}({\mathsf{s}},u)))^{2}}{\rm d}w+\oo(1)\right|,

(277)

where
z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u)
is outside the integration contour
{\omega_{-}}
. Note that
o(1)
in the last formula, which originates from
o(1)
in (
90
), is uniform in the sense of Remark
4.6
: it can be upper-bounded by a deterministic constant tending to
0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

By our assumption
({\mathsf{t}},u)
is an admissible pair (recall from Definition
6.2
). Hence, it follows from Lemmas
5.16
and
6.3
, that
|\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|
is uniformly bounded for
0\leqslant m\leqslant\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}
. Moreover,
{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u))
is bounded away from
{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}([\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{s}}),\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{s}})]
and we can take the contour such that
{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)
is bounded away from
{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}([\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{s}}),\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{s}})]
, thus, the last two factors in the integrands

\displaystyle\left|\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(w){\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u))}{({\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u)))^{2}}\right|,\left|\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(w){\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(\overline{z}({\mathsf{s}},u))}{({\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}(\overline{z}({\mathsf{s}},u)))^{2}}\right|

(278)

are uniformly bounded. In
Step 1
of Proposition
5.12
, we have studied the behavior of
\widetilde{f}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)
. It is uniformly bounded and bounded away from
1
, when
{\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{s}}}(w)
is bounded away from
{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{s}}([\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{s}}),\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{s}})]
. Thus on
{\omega_{-}}
,
|\tilde{f}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)/{\tilde{f}_{\mathsf{s}}(w)-1}|
is also uniformly bounded. Therefore, there exists a deterministic constant
C
(independent of
\varepsilon,m
) such that (
277
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2}\big|{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\right|\leqslant C\varepsilon.

(279)

Summing the above expression for
0\leqslant m\leqslant\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}
, we produce a deterministic bound on the random variable under the first expectation on the righthand side of (
276
):

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2}\,\big|\,{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\bigg)^{p}\leq C^{p}({\mathsf{t}}+\varepsilon)^{p}.

(280)

Switching to the second term in the righthand side of (
276
), thanks to Proposition
6.1
,
\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\big((z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\big)
is asymptotically Gaussian in the convergence of moments sense, and we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{p}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u))\big|^{2p}\,\big|\,{\bm{x}}(m)\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[|\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u))\big|^{2p}\,\big|\,{\bm{x}}(m)\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{2p}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}[\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\big]^{2p}+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigr]^{2p}\,\big|\,{\bm{x}}(m)\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{2p}(2p-1)!!\left(\mathbb{E}\big[\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}[\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\big]^{2}\big|{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\right)^{p}+\left(\mathbb{E}\big[\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\big]^{2}\big|{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\right)^{p}+\oo(1)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{2p+1}(2p-1)!!\left(\mathbb{E}\big[|\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u))|^{2}\,\big|\,{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\right)^{p}+\oo(1),

where in the second inequality we used that
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}[\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}]
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\varepsilon^{-1/2}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}]
are asymptotically Gaussian. Using the above estimate together with (
279
) and a uniform bound on
|\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|
, we estimate

\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2p}\big]\\
=|\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2p}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)|^{2p}\,\big|\,{\bm{x}}(m)\big]\right]\leq C^{\prime}\varepsilon^{p},

for a deterministic constant
C^{\prime}>0
. Summing this estimate for
0\leqslant m\leqslant\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}
, and recalling that
p\geq 1
, we conclude that the second term on the righthand side of (
276
) is uniformly bounded:

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)|^{2p}\big]\leqslant C^{\prime\prime}.

(281)

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)\right|^{2p}\leqslant C.

(282)

Plugging (
280
) and (
281
) into (
276
), we conclude that the sums in (
271
) have moments bounded uniformly in
\varepsilon
.
∎

We further show that the remainders
\mathcal{R}_{m}
in (
270
) do not contribute to the asymptotics.

Lemma 6.8
.

The random variables
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\left(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u)\right)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u)
have the same
\varepsilon\to 0
asymptotics (both in distribution and in the sense of moments) as the sums (
271
) in Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
. The
\varepsilon\to 0
convergence is uniform as in Theorem
6.5
.

Summing the result of Corollary
6.4
, we get for any
1\leqslant\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}\leqslant\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}
, we have

\frac{1}{\varepsilon}G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}\left(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u)\right)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u)=\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)+\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}\mathcal{R}_{m}^{\prime},

(283)

where, as a consequence of (
266
), the remainders satisfy for a constant
C_{1}>0
:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{m+1}^{\prime}|\leqslant C_{1}\bigl(\varepsilon+|G_{m}(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u))|+\mathbb{E}|G_{m}(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u))|\bigr).

(284)

A corollary of (
284
) and Hoelder’s inequality is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{R}_{m+1}^{\prime}|^{2p}\leqslant C_{2}\bigl(\varepsilon^{2p}+\mathbb{E}|G_{m}(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u))|^{2p}\bigr),\qquad p=1,2,\dots.

(285)

Next, dividing by
\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u)
and rising both sides of (
283
) to
2p
-th moments, we get

\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}/\varepsilon|^{2p}\right]\leqslant\frac{2^{2p}}{|\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{s}},u)|^{2p}}\Biggl(\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}\Delta{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\bigl(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u)\bigr)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m+\varepsilon,u)\right|^{2p}\\
+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}|\mathcal{R}_{m}^{\prime}|\right)^{2p}\Biggr)\leqslant C_{3}\left(1+(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}+1)^{2p-1}\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{R}_{m}^{\prime}|^{2p}\right)\\
\leqslant C_{4}\left(1+\varepsilon\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{R}_{m}^{\prime}/\varepsilon|^{2p}\right),

(286)

where in the second line we used (
282
) to bound the first term. By plugging (
285
) into (
286
), we get the following estimate,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}/\varepsilon|^{2p}\right]\leqslant C_{5}\left(1+\varepsilon\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}-1}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{m}/\varepsilon|^{2p}\right]\right).

(287)

We use (
287
) to prove by induction that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}-1}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{m}/\varepsilon^{-1}|^{p}\right]\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left((1+\varepsilon C_{5})^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}-1\right),

(288)

where we emphasize that the constants
C_{5}
in (
287
) and in (
288
) are the same.
The statement (
288
) holds for
{\mathsf{s}}=0
, because the sum is empty and the right-hand side of the inequality also vanishes. Next we prove it for
{\mathsf{s}}+\varepsilon
assuming that it holds for
{\mathsf{s}}
. Using the induction hypothesis, (
287
) gives that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}/\varepsilon|^{2p}\right]\leqslant C_{5}\left(1+\left((1+\varepsilon C_{5})^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}-1\right)\right)=C_{5}(1+\varepsilon C_{5})^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}.\end{split}

(289)

Combining (
288
) and (
289
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{m}/\varepsilon^{-1}|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left((1+\varepsilon C_{5})^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}-1\right)+C_{5}(1+\varepsilon C_{5})^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left((1+\varepsilon C_{5})^{\varepsilon^{-1}({\mathsf{s}}+1)}-1\right).

This finishes the induction. In particular, as a consequence of (
289
), we have a uniform bound:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{s}}}/\varepsilon|^{2p}\right]\leqslant C_{6},\qquad\text{for any }0\leqslant{\mathsf{s}}\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}.\end{split}

(290)

Once we know the uniform bound (
290
) we can obtain much better estimates on
\mathcal{R}_{m}
. Indeed, we recall that, in fact, according to (
266
), the constants in (
284
) and (
285
) tend to
0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. In other words, (
285
) can be replaced by a much stronger bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{R}_{m+1}^{\prime}|^{2p}\leqslant o(1)\cdot\bigl(\varepsilon^{2p}+\mathbb{E}|G_{m}(z^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon m,u))|^{2p}\bigr),\qquad p=1,2,\dots,\qquad\varepsilon\to 0.

(291)

Together with (
290
), this implies

\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{R}_{m}^{\prime}|^{2p}\leqslant o(\varepsilon^{2p}),

(292)

with implicit constant in
o(1)
being uniform over
m
. Therefore, for any
p=1,2,\dots
,

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{m=0}^{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\mathcal{R}_{m}^{\prime}\right|^{2p}\leq(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}+1)^{2p}\sup_{0\leq m\leq\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{R}_{m}^{\prime}|^{2p}=o(1).

(293)

Hence, the second sum in (
270
) does not contribute to
\varepsilon\to 0
asymptotics. On the other hand, the asymptotics of the first sum has already been analyzed in Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
.
∎

We can now finish the proof of Theorem
6.5
.

We will only prove the
k=1
statement, as the general case is the same.

Take an admissible pair
({\mathsf{t}},u)
and let
\mathfrak{z}=z({\mathsf{t}},u)
. Define
u^{\varepsilon}=[z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},\cdot)]^{-1}(\mathfrak{z})
, where the functional inverse is applied in the second argument of the function
z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},\cdot)
. Since
u\mapsto z({\mathsf{t}},u)
is a conformal bijection by Lemma
5.16
(in particular, its
u
–derivative is non-zero), using Lemma
6.3
we conclude that
u^{\varepsilon}
is well-defined for all small-enough
\varepsilon
and
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}u^{\varepsilon}=u
.

Lemmas
6.6
,
6.7
,
6.8
together with Lemma
6.3
imply that
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\left(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u^{\varepsilon})\right)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u^{\varepsilon})
is asymptotically Gaussian (in the sense of convergence of the moments), with the variance given by (
269
), in which you replace
z({\mathsf{t}},u)
by
\mathfrak{z}
. Because
z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u^{\varepsilon})=\mathfrak{z}=z({\mathsf{t}},u)
and
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u^{\varepsilon})=\partial_{u}z({\mathsf{t}},u)
by Lemma
6.3
, the convergence of
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\left(z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u^{\varepsilon})\right)\partial_{u}z^{\varepsilon}({\mathsf{t}},u^{\varepsilon})
is equivalent to the desired convergence of
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}}\left(z({\mathsf{t}},u)\right)\partial_{u}z({\mathsf{t}},u)
and the limits are the same.
∎

6.3
Matching the covariance structure with the Gaussian Free Field

In this section we finish the proof of Theorem
1.11
. Theorem
6.5
implies that the fluctuations of the height function of tilings are asymptotically Gaussian and it remains to match the covariance structure with that of the Gaussian Free Field. We only present the details under Assumption
5.11
:
0<{\mathsf{q}}<1
and large real
\kappa
.

Our first step is to recast the result of Theorem
6.5
in terms of the height functions. In parallel with (
260
), we introduce a function
w({\mathsf{t}},w_{0}):={\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z({\mathsf{t}},{\mathsf{b}}_{0}^{-1}(w_{0}))
, which maps
w_{0}
to
w^{\mathsf{t}}
and is given more explicitly by (
225
).

Corollary 6.9
.

In the setting of Theorem
1.11
, fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
times
0<{\mathsf{t}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\leqslant\dots\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}_{k}<{\mathsf{T}}
and
k
real-valued functions
f_{1}(x),\dots,f_{k}(x)
, such that
f_{i}(x)=\partial_{x}[F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(x))]
and
F_{i}(x)
is analytic in a complex neighborhood of the real line
\mathbb{R}
. Then the random vector

\left(\sqrt{\pi}\int_{\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}^{\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}f_{i}()\bigl(h(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i},\varepsilon^{-1})-\mathbb{E}[h(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i},\varepsilon^{-1})]\bigr){\rm d}\right)_{i=1}^{k},

(294)

converges in the sense of moments to a centered Gaussian vector with covariance of
i
–th and
j
–th components given by

-\frac{1}{4\pi}\oint_{{\mathcal{C}}_{i}}\oint_{{\mathcal{C}}_{j}}\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{w_{j}}\bigl[\log\left(w_{i}-w_{j}\right)\bigr]\,F_{i}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},w_{i}))F_{j}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{j},w_{j}))\,{\rm d}w_{i}{\rm d}w_{j},

(295)

where the positively oriented contour
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
in the complex plane is chosen so that its
w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},\cdot)
–image in the
w
–plane is a simple curve enclosing
{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}([\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{t}}_{i}),\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})])
and staying in the neighborhood where
F_{i}
is analytic; similarly for
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
. If we assume that
t_{i}\leqslant t_{j}
, then
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
should be inside
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
.

Remark 6.10
.

It is plausible that a version of Corollary
6.9
holds for general analytic
f_{i}(x)
, i.e. the more restrictive form
f_{i}(x)={\mathsf{b}}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{t}}(x)F_{i}^{\prime}({\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(x))
is not necessary. A technical difficulty in proving such an extension is the singular behavior of the flow
z^{\mathsf{t}}
, as in (
260
), at the corners of the domain
{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathsf{t}}
, i.e. at the points
{\mathsf{t}}/2-\log_{\mathsf{q}}(\kappa)
and
{\mathsf{t}}/2-\log_{\mathsf{q}}(\kappa)-\mathbf{i}\frac{\pi}{\ln{\mathsf{q}}}
, where
f_{\mathsf{t}}(z)
equals
1
. We do not have to deal with the flow going through these points (which is an obstacle for applying Theorem
6.5
), if we use the more restrictive choice of
f_{i}(x)
, as in Corollary
6.9
.

We recall the relation between the height function and the empirical density of the particles used in the definition (
250
) of
G_{t}(z)
:

\varepsilon h(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}},\varepsilon^{-1})=\int_{-\infty}\rho(s,{\bm{x}}(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}})){\rm d}s.

Hence, integrating by parts,
using
(
94
) and (
250
) we have

\int_{\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}^{\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}f_{i}()\bigl(h(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i},\varepsilon^{-1})-\mathbb{E}[h(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i},\varepsilon^{-1})]\bigr){\rm d}\\
=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}^{\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(s))\bigl(\rho(s,{\bm{x}}(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}))-\mathbb{E}[\rho(s,{\bm{x}}(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}))]\bigr){\rm d}s=\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\oint_{\Upsilon_{i}}F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z))G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z){\rm d}z,

(296)

where
\Upsilon_{i}
is the contour shown in Figure
9
. The main feature of this contour is that it encloses the
[\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{t}}_{i}),\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})]
interval and no other singularities of
G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z)
; in addition its
{\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(\cdot)
–image stays close to the real line, guaranteeing that
F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z))
is holomorphic everywhere inside the contour. The contour depends on a large negative real parameter
M_{i}
and consists of two parts: the first part (shown in solid in Figure
9
) starts at
M_{i}-\mathbf{i}\pi/\ln{\mathsf{q}}
on the top border of
{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathsf{t}}
, stays inside
{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathsf{t}}
and closely follows its boundary until reaching
M_{i}
, and then proceeds in the same way from
M_{i}
to
M_{i}+\mathbf{i}\pi/\ln{\mathsf{q}}
staying inside
\overline{{\mathbb{D}}}_{\mathsf{t}}
. The second part (shown dashed in Figure
9
) returns from
M_{i}+\mathbf{i}\pi/\ln{\mathsf{q}}
to
M_{i}-\mathbf{i}\pi/\ln{\mathsf{q}}
by following the three straight lines of the boundary of
{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathsf{t}}\cup\overline{{\mathbb{D}}}_{\mathsf{t}}
.

Fig 9:
Top panel: contour
\Upsilon_{i}
; the integral over the dashed part vanishes. Bottom panel: contour
{\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(\Upsilon_{i})
has two parts; the solid part is
w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},\cdot)
–image of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
.

Observe that the part of the integral (
296
) over the dashed contour in Figure
9
vanishes: indeed the integral over its part in the upper halfplane is minus the integral over its part in the lower half-plane, as follows from the identities:

F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z+2\mathbf{i}\pi/\ln{\mathsf{q}}))G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z+2\mathbf{i}\pi/\ln{\mathsf{q}})=F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z))G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z)

and for
r\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}({\mathsf{t}}_{i}/2-\log_{\mathsf{q}}\kappa+\mathbf{i}r))G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}({\mathsf{t}}_{i}/2-\log_{\mathsf{q}}\kappa+\mathbf{i}r)

\displaystyle=-F_{i}({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}({\mathsf{t}}_{i}/2-\log_{\mathsf{q}}\kappa-\mathbf{i}r))G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}({\mathsf{t}}_{i}/2-\log_{\mathsf{q}}\kappa-\mathbf{i}r).

Hence, it is sufficient to only integrate in (
296
) over the solid part of the
\Upsilon_{i}
–contour, which we denote
\Upsilon_{i}^{s}
; the
{\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}
–image of
\Upsilon_{i}^{s}
is a simple closed loop, as in the bottom panel of Figure
9
.

Lemma
5.16
, Remark
5.17
, and the identity
z({\mathsf{t}},\bar{u})=\overline{z({\mathsf{t}},u)}

guarantee that we can find a contour
\Upsilon^{0}_{i}
in the
{\mathbb{D}}_{0}\cup\bar{\mathbb{D}}_{0}
inside the
u
–plane, such that
\Upsilon_{i}^{c}
is its image under
u\mapsto z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},w_{0})
. We can then change variables
z=z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i})
in (
296
) converting it into

\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\oint_{\Upsilon_{i}^{0}}F_{i}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}))\bigr)G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}))\partial_{u_{i}}z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}){\rm d}u_{i}.

(297)

The joint moments of the integrals (
297
) are readily reduced to the integrals of the joint moments of random variables
G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}))\partial_{u_{i}}z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i})
, which were computed in Theorem
6.5
. Hence, applying this theorem (all the points
({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i})
,
u_{i}\in\Upsilon^{0}_{i}
, are admissible as long as
M_{i}
is large enough), we conclude that the integrals (
297
) are asymptotically Gaussian (jointly over
i=1,\dots,k
) with covariance given by

-\frac{\varepsilon^{-2}}{4\pi^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\oint_{\Upsilon_{i}^{0}}F_{i}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}))\bigr)G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}))\partial_{u_{i}}z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}){\rm d}u_{i}\\
\times\oint_{\Upsilon_{j}^{0}}F_{j}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{j},u_{j}))\bigr)G_{\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{j},u_{j}))\partial_{u_{i}}z({\mathsf{t}}_{j},u_{j}){\rm d}u_{j}\Biggr]\to\\
-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\oint_{\Upsilon_{i}^{0}}\oint_{\Upsilon_{j}^{0}}\partial_{u_{i}}\partial_{u_{j}}\ln\left[\frac{{\mathsf{b}}_{0}(u_{i})-{\mathsf{b}}_{0}(u_{j})}{{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{i})\bigr)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{j})\bigr)}\right]F_{i}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{i},u_{i}))\bigr)F_{j}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{j},u_{j}))\bigr){\rm d}u_{j}{\rm d}u_{i},

(298)

where
\tau=\min({\mathsf{t}}_{i},{\mathsf{t}}_{j})
. We split the logarithm of the ratio in (
298
) into the sum of two logarithms. The term involving
{\mathsf{b}}_{0}
matches (
295
), if we change the variables
w_{i}={\mathsf{b}}_{0}(u_{i})
, denote
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}={\mathsf{b}}_{0}(\Upsilon_{i}^{0})
and take into account
\sqrt{\pi}
prefactor in (
294
). It remains to show that the term involving
{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}
vanishes.

Up to now we have not used the nesting condition for
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
contours, and it becomes important at this point. We now assume
t_{i}\leqslant t_{j}
, so that
\tau=t_{i}
and we also assume that
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
is inside
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
, which implies
M_{i}>M_{j}
. The part of (
298
) involving
{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}
is transformed into

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\oint_{\Upsilon_{i}^{0}}\oint_{\Upsilon_{j}^{0}}&\frac{\partial_{u_{i}}[z(u_{i},\tau)]{\mathsf{b}}^{\prime}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{i})\bigr)\cdot\partial_{u_{j}}[z(\tau,u_{j})]\,{\mathsf{b}}^{\prime}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{j})\bigr)}{\left[{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{i})\bigr)-{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{j})\bigr)\right]^{2}}\\
&\times F_{i}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(z(\tau,u_{i}))\bigr)F_{j}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}(z({\mathsf{t}}_{j},u_{j}))\bigr){\rm d}u_{j}{\rm d}u_{i}.\end{split}

(299)

We isolate in the last integral the
u_{i}
–dependent factors and change the variable
v={\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}(z(\tau,u_{i}))
in this part, getting

\oint_{w({\mathsf{t}},{\mathcal{C}}_{i})}\frac{F_{i}(v)}{\left[v-{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{j})\bigr)\right]^{2}}{\rm d}v,

(300)

where the integration contour
{w({\mathsf{t}},{\mathcal{C}}_{i})}
is precisely the solid contour in the bottom panel of Figure
9
. Because
{\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}(u_{j})
belong to
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
, it is outside
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
; hence,
{\mathsf{b}}_{\tau}\bigl(z(\tau,u_{j}))
is outside the contour
w({\mathsf{t}},{\mathcal{C}}_{i})
(the map
w_{0}\mapsto w({\mathsf{t}},w_{0})
preserves nesting, because (
233
) moves all points up). Therefore, the integrand in (
300
) has no singularities inside the integration contour and the integrals in (
300
) and in (
299
) vanish.
∎

Next, we deal with the map
\Omega
and the associated pullback of the Gaussian Free Field.

We recall that
\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
are the (open) upper and lower halfplanes. We also recall that the liquid region
{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{P}})
consists of those
({\mathsf{t}},)
, for which the equation (
19
) has non-real roots
u
, see Propositions
5.18
and
5.19
. There are
4
such roots, leading to two values of
{\mathsf{q}}^{-u}+\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{u}
. We let
u=u({\mathsf{t}},)
be the root in
{\mathbb{D}}_{0}^{\circ}
and set
\Omega({\mathsf{t}},)={\mathsf{q}}^{-u}+\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{u}
corresponding to this root. Then
\Omega
is in the lower half-plane
\mathbb{H}^{-}
and its conjugate
\bar{\Omega}
is in
\mathbb{H}^{+}
. Let us repeat and prove Lemma
1.9
:

Lemma 6.11
.

The map
({\mathsf{t}},)\mapsto\Omega({\mathsf{t}},)
is a bijection between the liquid region
{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{P}})
and
\mathbb{H}^{-}
.

We take
\omega\in\mathbb{H}^{-}
and show that there exists
(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{P}})
, such that
\omega=\Omega(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)
. As in Section
5.4
, we consider the dynamics
w^{\mathsf{t}}
of (
225
) started at
w^{0}=\omega
: equivalently, this is
w^{\mathsf{t}}={\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}(z^{\mathsf{t}})
with
z^{\mathsf{t}}
solving (
218
). We recall that by (
227
), the imaginary part of
w^{\mathsf{t}}
is a continuous monotonously increasing function of
{\mathsf{t}}
. By our choice of
\omega
,
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(w^{0})<0
. On the other hand, by (
234
),
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(w^{\mathsf{T}})>0
. Hence, there is a unique choice of
0<\hat{\mathsf{t}}<{\mathsf{T}}
, for which
w^{\hat{\mathsf{t}}}
is real. The equation
{\mathsf{q}}^{-}+\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-\hat{\mathsf{t}}}=w^{\hat{\mathsf{t}}}
, as an equation on , has two real roots and we choose the smaller one, which matches
z^{\tilde{\mathsf{t}}}
. Then
(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{P}})
, as we explained right above Lemma
5.16
and in the proof of Proposition
5.19
. On the other hand, if we express
w^{\hat{\mathsf{t}}}
through
u
, using (
225
) and
w^{0}={\mathsf{q}}^{-u}+\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{u}
, then
{\mathsf{q}}^{-}+\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-\hat{\mathsf{t}}}=w^{\hat{\mathsf{t}}}(u)
becomes (
19
) as an equation for
u
. Therefore,
\Omega(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)={\mathsf{q}}^{-u}+\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{u}=w^{0}=\omega
, as desired.

We also need to show that
(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)
satisfying
\omega=\Omega(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)
is unique. Indeed, given
(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)
, we construct
\omega
using (
19
). But then comparison of (
225
) with (
19
) (they are the same) shows that the flow
w^{\mathsf{t}}
,
{\mathsf{t}}\geqslant 0
, started at
w^{0}=\omega
hits the real axis precisely at
w^{\hat{\mathsf{t}}}={\mathsf{q}}^{-}+\kappa^{2}{\mathsf{q}}^{-\hat{\mathsf{t}}}
; the hitting time uniquely determines
\hat{\mathsf{t}}
and the hitting point uniquely determines .

Alternatively, the same uniqueness can be proven by looking directly at (
19
) and noticing that once we fix
u
, the value of
{\mathsf{t}}
is uniquely reconstructed by taking the imaginary part of (
19
) and noticing that the left-hand side is real. Once we know both
u
and
{\mathsf{t}}
, the value of is also readily reconstructed from (
19
).
∎

Definition 6.12
.

We let
((\omega),{\mathsf{t}}(\omega))
be the inverse map to
\Omega({\mathsf{t}},)
.
For each
0<\hat{\mathsf{t}}<{\mathsf{T}}
we let

\gamma_{\hat{\mathsf{t}}}=\{\Omega(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)\mid(\hat{\mathsf{t}},)\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{P}})\}=\{\omega\in\mathbb{H}^{-}\mid{\mathsf{t}}(\omega)=\hat{\mathsf{t}}\}.

Because the map
\Omega
is defined in terms of algebraic equations,
\gamma_{\mathsf{t}}
is a union of finitely many simple curves. These curves can not be closed, as that would contradict
\Omega
being bijective; they also can not end at non-real points, since by implicit function theorem, we are always able to slightly extend each curve around each of its non-real points. We conclude that
\gamma_{\mathsf{t}}
is a union of finitely many curves, which start and end on the real axis.

As the final ingredient, we define the Gaussian Free Field.

Definition 6.13
.

The Gaussian Free Field in
\mathbb{H}^{-}
(or in
\mathbb{H}^{+}
) with Dirichlet boundary conditions is a generalized centered Gaussian field
\mathbf{GFF}(z)
,
z\in\mathbb{H}^{-}
(or
z\in\mathbb{H}^{+}
) with covariance

\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\mathbf{GFF}(z),\mathbf{GFF}(w)\bigr)=K(z,w)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\left|\frac{z-w}{z-\bar{w}}\right|.

(301)

Formally, it is defined in terms of its pairings with test-measures: for a signed (real) measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{H}^{-}
(or
\mathbb{H}^{+}
), such that

\iint K(z,w)\mu({\rm d}z)\mu({\rm d}w)<\infty,

we define a mean
0
Gaussian random variable
\langle\mathbf{GFF},\mu\rangle
, so that for several such
\mu
’s the joint distribution of pairings is a Gaussian vector with covariance

\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\langle\mathbf{GFF},\mu\rangle,\langle\mathbf{GFF},\tilde{\mu}\rangle\bigr)=\iint K(z,w)\mu({\rm d}z)\tilde{\mu}({\rm d}w).

(302)

We refer to
[
99
,
105
]
, and
[
57
, Lecture 11]
for the more detailed definition and discussion of the GFF. We remark that GFF can be identified with a random element of a certain Sobolev space (this is in parallel with the Brownian motion, which is first defined in terms of its covariance, and the recast as a random continuous function).

Let us also specialize the definition of the Gaussian Free Field to two types of signed measures
\mu
. First, if
\mu
is absolutely continuous and has (signed) density
\mu(x+\mathbf{i}y)
(and similarly for
\tilde{\mu}
), then (
302
) becomes

\int\int\int\int K(x+\mathbf{i}y,\tilde{x}+\mathbf{i}\tilde{y})\mu(x+\mathbf{i}y)\tilde{\mu}(\tilde{x}+\mathbf{i}\tilde{y})\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}y\,{\rm d}\tilde{x}\,{\rm d}\tilde{y}.

Second, if
\mu
is supported on a curve
\gamma
and has density
\mu(z)
along this curve (and similarly for
\tilde{\mu}
), then (
302
) becomes

\int_{\gamma}\int_{\tilde{\gamma}}K(z,w)\mu(z)\tilde{\mu}(\tilde{z})\,|{\rm d}z||{\rm d}w|.

Proposition 6.14
.

The asymptotic covariance (
295
) in Corollary
6.9
can be rewritten using Definitions
6.12
and
6.13
as

\int_{{}_{i}\in\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}}\int_{{}_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}}K\bigl(\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i}),\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})\bigr)f_{j}(_{j})f_{i}(_{i}){\rm d}_{j}{\rm d}_{i}=\\
\int_{\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}}\int_{\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}}K(w_{i},w_{j})\cdot\bigl[f_{j}((w_{j}))^{\prime}(w_{j}){\rm d}w_{j}\bigr]\cdot\bigl[f_{i}((w_{i}))^{\prime}(w_{i}){\rm d}w_{i}\bigr],

(303)

where
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}}
in the second formula is the liquid region at time
{\mathsf{t}}
, as in (
232
);
{}^{\prime}(w_{i}){\rm d}w_{i}
in the second formula should be understood as the directional derivative of
(w_{i})
along the
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}
curve multiplied by
|{\rm d}w_{i}|
; the equivalence of two forms in (
303
) is by change of variables.

We assume
{\mathsf{t}}_{i}\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}_{j}
throughout the proof and recall that (
295
) reads

-\frac{1}{4\pi}\oint_{{\mathcal{C}}_{i}}\oint_{{\mathcal{C}}_{j}}\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{w_{j}}\bigl[\log\left(w_{i}-w_{j}\right)\bigr]\,F_{i}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},w_{i}))F_{j}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{j},w_{j}))\,{\rm d}w_{i}{\rm d}w_{j},

(304)

We deform the contour
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
into
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}
and deform the contour
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
into
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}
in (
304
); the involved contours are schematically shown in Figure
10
. Let us explain that this deformation is possible and does not change the value on the integral. Because as
{\mathsf{t}}
grows the points
w^{{\mathsf{t}}}
move up (recall (
225
)) until they reach the real axis and the curves
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}
,
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}
are disjoint by Lemma
6.11
,
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}
is situated below
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}
. In other words, the union of the simple closed contours
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}
is inside the union of the simple contours of
\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}
, matching the same configuration for
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
. Hence, the factor
\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{w_{j}}\log(w_{i}-w_{j})=\frac{1}{(w_{i}-w_{j})^{2}}
remains non-singular in the deformation and does not create any residues.

Fig 10:
The action of
w^{0}\mapsto w^{{\mathsf{t}}}
map on the contours of integration in the situation when the liquid region has two segments.

For the remaining two factors
F_{i}(\cdot)
and
F_{j}(\cdot)
, we use the fact that
w({\mathsf{t}},w_{0})
is a holomorphic function of
w_{0}
outside
\gamma_{\mathsf{t}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}}
. This fact follows from Lemma
5.16
: the Lemma gives holomorphicity in the lower half-plane; conjugating, we also get holomorphicity in the upper half-plane, and applying the reflection principle we get holomorphicity on the real axis outside
\gamma_{\mathsf{t}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}}
, cf. Figure
10
.

We conclude that no poles are encountered in the deformation and transform (
304
) into

-\frac{1}{4\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}}\oint_{\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}\cup\bar{\gamma}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}}\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{w_{j}}\bigl[\ln(w_{i}-w_{j}))\bigr]\,F_{i}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},w_{i}))F_{j}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{j},w_{j}))\,{\rm d}w_{i}{\rm d}w_{j}.

(305)

Note that
w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},w_{i})=w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},\bar{w}_{i})
is real on the integration contour and so is
F_{i}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},w_{i}))
(because
f_{i}
and
F_{i}
were required to be real on the real line). Similarly,
F_{j}(w({\mathsf{t}}_{j},w_{j}))
is real. Hence,
F_{i}
and
F_{j}
factors do not change when we conjugate the variables. Therefore, differentiating the logarithm explicitly, splitting (
305
) into four integrals, corresponding to upper and lower half-plane parts of
w_{i}
and
w_{j}
contours and recombining, we get an alternative expression:

-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}}\int_{\gamma_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}}\left(\frac{{\rm d}w_{i}{\rm d}w_{j}}{(w_{i}-w_{j})^{2}}-\frac{{\rm d}\bar{w}_{i}{\rm d}w_{j}}{(\bar{w}_{i}-w_{j})^{2}}-\frac{{\rm d}w_{i}{\rm d}\bar{w}_{j}}{(w_{i}-\bar{w}_{j})^{2}}+\frac{{\rm d}\bar{w}_{i}{\rm d}\bar{w}_{j}}{(\bar{w}_{i}-\bar{w}_{j})^{2}}\right)F_{i}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}((w_{i}))\bigr)F_{j}\bigl({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}((w_{j}))\bigr),

(306)

where we also identified
{\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}((w_{i}))=w({\mathsf{t}}_{i},w_{i})
and
{\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}((w_{j}))=w({\mathsf{t}}_{j},w_{j})
. We change the variables
{}_{i}=(w_{i})
,
{}_{j}=(w_{j})
, notice that
w_{i}=\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})
and
w_{j}=\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})
, and then integrate by parts in
i
and in
j
to get

-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}}\int_{\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}}\ln\left[\frac{\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}{\bar{\Omega}({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}\cdot\frac{\bar{\Omega}({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\bar{\Omega}({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}{\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\bar{\Omega}({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}\right]f_{i}(_{i})f_{j}(_{j}){\rm d}_{j}{\rm d}_{i}.

(307)

Note that there are no additional terms in the integration by parts, because
w_{i}
is real at the end-points of connected components of
\gamma_{i}
and
w_{j}
is real at the end-points of connected components of
\gamma_{j}
. (
307
) matches (
303
).
∎

We have now collected all ingredients of Theorem
1.11
and can state its precise form.

Theorem 6.15
.

Consider the limit regime (
10
) and assume that we are in the real or imaginary case. Fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
times
0<{\mathsf{t}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\leqslant\dots\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}_{k}<{\mathsf{T}}
and
k
real functions
f_{1}(x),\dots,f_{k}(x)
, such that
f_{i}(x)=\partial_{x}\bigl[F({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(x))\bigr]
for a real-analytic function
F
. Then the random vector

\left(\sqrt{\pi}\int_{\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}^{\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{t}}_{i})}f_{i}()\bigl(h(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i},\varepsilon^{-1})-\mathbb{E}[h(\varepsilon^{-1}{\mathsf{t}}_{i},\varepsilon^{-1})]\bigr){\rm d}\right)_{i=1}^{k},

converges in the sense of moments to a centered Gaussian vector with covariance of
i
–th and
j
–th components given by

\int_{{}_{i}\in\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}}\int_{{}_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{j}}}\left[-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\left|\frac{\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}{\bar{\Omega}({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}\right|\right]f_{j}(_{j})f_{i}(_{i}){\rm d}_{j}{\rm d}_{i},

(308)

where
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathsf{t}}}=\{\mid({\mathsf{t}},)\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{P}})\}
is the section of the liquid region at time
{\mathsf{t}}
, as in (
232
), and
\Omega
is the bijection of Lemmas
1.9
and
6.11
.

Remark 6.16
.

Since
-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\left|\frac{\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}{\bar{\Omega}({\mathsf{t}}_{i},_{i})-\Omega({\mathsf{t}}_{j},_{j})}\right|
is the composition of the covariance of GFF in
\mathbb{H}^{+}
given by (
23
) or (
302
) with the map
\bar{\Omega}({\mathsf{t}},)
, (
308
) is the covariance of the pairings with
\Omega
–pullback of the Gaussian Free Field, as claimed in Theorem
1.11
. By integrating the result of Theorem
6.15
in
{\mathsf{t}}
–direction, we can also extend the convergence statement to pairings of GFF with real test-functions
f({\mathsf{t}},)
of the form
f({\mathsf{t}},)=\partial_{x}\bigl[F({\mathsf{t}},{\mathsf{b}}_{\mathsf{t}}())\bigr]
, where
F
is analytic in --variable and continuous in
{\mathsf{t}}
–variable.

Remark 6.17
.

The functions of the form
f(x)=\partial_{x}\bigl[F({\mathsf{b}}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{i}}(x))\bigr]
with real-analytic
F(\cdot)
are dense in the space of all continuous function on
[\mathbf{l}({\mathsf{t}}),\mathbf{r}({\mathsf{t}})]
equipped with the uniform convergence topology; hence, these test–functions are sufficient to uniquely determine the limiting Gaussian field.

This is combination of Corollary
6.9
with Proposition
6.14
.
∎

A
Proofs of claims in Section
3

We recall from
[
100
]
,

[
82
, Chapter VI: (7.14’), (6.11), and Section 10]
, and
[
93
, (2.3)]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&J_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(1;\theta)=\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{f(y_{i}-y_{j})f(x_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta)}{f(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta)f(x_{i}-y_{j})},\qquad f(x)=\frac{\Gamma(x+1)}{\Gamma(x+\theta)};\\
&J_{\bm{\mu}}(\bm{1};\theta)=\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left[\frac{\Gamma(y_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma(y_{i}-y_{j})}\cdot\frac{\Gamma(\theta(j-i))}{\Gamma(\theta(j-i+1))}\right].\end{split}

(309)

Plugging (
309
) into (
50
) and ignoring the factors which do not depend on
{\bm{y}}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\bm{y}}|{\bm{x}})\propto\prod_{i<j}(y_{i}-y_{j})\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j+1})\Gamma(x_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)\Gamma(x_{i}-y_{j}+1)}.

(310)

This finishes the proof of (
51
). In the following we show that (
310
) is a special case of (
27
).
We recall the general form of the transition probability (
27
):

\mathbb{P}({\bm{y}}|{\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{Z({\bm{x}})}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\bigl(b(y_{i})-b(y_{j})\bigr)\,\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[w(y_{i})\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1}{b(\ell)-b(y_{i})}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1}{b(y_{i})-b(\ell)}\right].

(311)

Let us take
b(z)=z
in (
311
). The second factor becomes
\prod_{i<j}(y_{i}-y_{j})
.
For the product over
\ell>y_{i}
in the third factor in (
311
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}(\ell-y_{i})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[\prod_{\ell=y_{i}+1}^{x_{i}}(\ell-y_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\prod_{\ell=x_{j+1}+\theta}^{x_{j}}(\ell-y_{i})\right]\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[\Gamma(x_{i}-y_{i}+1)\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\frac{\Gamma(x_{j}-y_{i}+1)}{\Gamma(x_{j+1}-y_{i}+\theta)}\right]=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\Gamma(x_{1}-y_{j}+\theta)\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma(x_{i}-y_{j}+1)}{\Gamma(x_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)},\end{split}

(312)

where we exchanged the roles of
i
and
j
in the last equality.
Similarly, for the
\ell<y_{i}
factor we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}(y_{i}-\ell)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[\prod_{\ell=x_{i+1}+\theta}^{y_{i}-1}(y_{i}-\ell)\prod_{j=n}^{i+1}\prod_{\ell=x_{j+1}+\theta}^{x_{j}}(y_{i}-\ell)\right]\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{i+1}-\theta+1)\prod_{j=i+1}^{n}\frac{\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)}{\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j})}\right]\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{n+1})\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)}{\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j+1})}.\end{split}

(313)

Plugging (
312
) and (
313
) into (
311
), we get
\mathbb{P}({\bm{y}}|{\bm{x}})
is proportional to

\displaystyle\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(y_{i}-y_{j})\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma(x_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j+1})}{\Gamma(x_{i}-y_{j}+1)\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{w(y_{i})}{\Gamma(x_{1}-y_{i}+\theta)\Gamma(y_{i}-x_{n+1})},

which simplifies to (
310
) if

w(z)={\Gamma(x_{1}-z+\theta)}{\Gamma(z-x_{n+1})}.

∎

The evaluation of the Macdonald polynomial under the principle specialization
\rho=(1,t,\dots,t^{n-1})
is explicit, see
[
82
, Chapter VI, (6.11)]
:

\displaystyle P_{\bm{\lambda}}(1,t,\dots,t^{n-1};q,t)=t^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(i-1)\lambda_{i}}\prod_{i<j\leqslant n}\frac{(q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}{(t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}},

(314)

for
{\bm{\lambda}}=\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\dots\geqslant\lambda_{n}\geqslant 0
;
P_{\bm{\lambda}}(\rho;q,t)=0
if
\lambda_{n+1}>0
. We further use the
w_{u,v}
automorphism of the algebra of symmetric functions
\Lambda
defined on the power sums by:

w_{u,v}(p_{k})=(-1)^{k-1}\frac{1-u^{k}}{1-v^{k}}p_{k}.

As shown in
[
82
, Chapter VI, Section 7]
,

w_{t,q}\bigl(P_{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}/{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}}(\cdot;t,q)\bigr)=Q_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(\cdot;q,t),

where
{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}
and
{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}
are transposed Young diagrams
{\bm{\lambda}}
and
{\bm{\mu}}
, respectively. Hence, with
\rho^{\prime}
being the specialization
\beta_{1}=a
, as in (
61
), we have

Q_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(\beta_{1}=a;q,t)=P_{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}/{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}}(\alpha_{1}=a;t,q).

The latter specialization of the skew
P
–function in one variable is
[
82
, Chapter VI, (7.14’)]
; in the notations there, it is
a^{|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|}\psi_{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}/{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}}(t,q)=a^{|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|}\psi^{\prime}_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(q,t)
with an additional requirement that
{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}\prec{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}
. The latter is the same as requiring

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}=\mu_{i}+e_{i},\quad e_{i}\in\{0,1\},\quad 1\leqslant i\leqslant n.

(315)

The final formula is

\displaystyle Q_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(\beta_{1}=a;q,t)

\displaystyle=a^{|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|}\prod_{i<j\leqslant n}\bm{1}(e_{i}=0,e_{j}=1)\frac{(1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i-1})(1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}-1}t^{j-i+1})}{(1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i})(1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}-1}t^{j-i})}.

Let us also rewrite (
314
) in terms of
{\bm{\mu}}
and
{\bm{e}}
using (
315
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}P_{\bm{\lambda}}(1,t,\dots,t^{n-1};q,t)=t^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)\mu_{i}}t^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)e_{i}}\prod_{i<j\leqslant n}\frac{(q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}+e_{i}-e_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}+e_{i}-e_{j}}t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}{(t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}\\
&=t^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(i-1)\mu_{i}}\prod_{i<j\leqslant n}\Biggl[\frac{(q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}{(t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}t^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)e_{i}}\\
&\left(\bm{1}(e_{i}=0,e_{j}=1)\frac{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}-1}t^{j-i}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}-1}t^{j-i+1}}+\bm{1}(e_{i}=1,e_{j}=0)\frac{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i}}+\bm{1}(e_{i}=e_{j})\right)\Biggr].\end{split}

Combining the last two formulas together we convert (
60
) into

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}({\bm{\lambda}}\mid{\bm{\mu}})\propto a^{|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|}t^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)e_{i}}\times\\
&\prod_{i<j}\left[\bm{1}(e_{i}=0,e_{j}=1)\frac{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i-1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i}}+\bm{1}(e_{i}=1,e_{j}=0)\frac{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i}}+\bm{1}(e_{i}=e_{j})\right],\end{split}

(316)

with additional requirement (
315
).
Setting
t=q^{\theta}
and encoding the Young diagrams
{\bm{\mu}}
and
{\bm{\lambda}}
as particle systems
{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{W}_{\theta}^{n}
and
{\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}\in\mathbb{W}_{\theta}^{n}
, we rewrite the transition probability (
316
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}\mid{\bm{x}})\propto a^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e_{i}}t^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)e_{i}}\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-q^{x_{i}-x_{j}+\theta(e_{i}-e_{j})}}{1-q^{x_{i}-x_{j}}}\propto a^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e_{i}}\prod_{i<j}\frac{q^{x_{i}+\theta e_{i}}-q^{x_{j}+\theta e_{j}}}{q^{x_{i}}-q^{x_{j}}},

which is a special case of (
4
), with
b(z)=q^{z}
,
\phi^{-}(z)=1
, and
\phi^{+}(z)=a
.
∎

Using
[
82
, Chapter VI, (7.14’)]
and notations (
59
), we have

\displaystyle P_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(t^{n};q,t)=t^{n(|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|)}f(1)^{n}\prod_{i<j\leqslant n}f(q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i})\prod_{i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{f(q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j+1}}t^{j-i})}{f(q^{\mu_{i}-\lambda_{j+1}}t^{j-i})f(q^{\lambda_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{j-i})}.

(317)

Plugging (
317
) and (
314
) and setting
t=q^{\theta}
, we rewrite the transition probability in Definition
3.9
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\bm{y}}|{\bm{x}})\propto q^{-\theta\sum_{i=1}^{n}(n-i+1)y_{i}}\prod_{i<j\leqslant n}(1-q^{y_{i}-y_{j}})\prod_{i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1})}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1}+1-\theta)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+1)},

(318)

which matches (
65
).
In the following we show that (
318
) is a special case of (
27
), with
b(z)=q^{-z}
. We recall the transition probability (
27
)

\mathbb{P}({\bm{y}}|{\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{Z({\bm{x}})}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\bigl(b(y_{i})-b(y_{j})\bigr)\,\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[w(y_{i})\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1}{b(\ell)-b(y_{i})}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1}{b(y_{i})-b(\ell)}\right].

(319)

We take
b(z)=q^{-z}
in (
319
). The second factor becomes,

\displaystyle\prod_{i<j}(b(y_{i})-b(y_{j}))=\prod_{i<j}(q^{-y_{i}}-q^{-y_{j}})=\prod_{i<j}(1-q^{y_{i}-y_{j}})q^{-y_{i}}=\prod_{i<j}(1-q^{y_{i}-y_{j}})\prod_{i}q^{-(n-i)y_{i}}.

(320)

For the last product in (
319
), we have

\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\bigl(b(\ell)-b(y_{i})\bigr)\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\bigl(b(y_{i})-b(\ell)\bigr)\\
\propto\left(\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1-q^{\ell-y_{i}}}{1-q}\right)\left(\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1-q^{y_{i}-\ell}}{1-q}\right)\left(\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}q^{-\ell}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}q^{-y_{i}}\right).

(321)

We transform the first factor in the righthand side of (
321
) as

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{\ell=y_{i}+1}^{x_{i}}\frac{1-q^{\ell-y_{i}}}{1-q}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\left[\prod_{\ell=x_{j+1}+\theta}^{x_{j}}\frac{1-q^{\ell-y_{i}}}{1-q}\right]\right)\\
=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{i}+1)\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{j}-y_{i}+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{j+1}+\theta-y_{i})}\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{q}(x_{1}-y_{i}+\theta)\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)},

(322)

where in the last identity we swapped
i
and
j
. We also transform the second factor in (
321
):

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{\ell=x_{i+1}+\theta}^{y_{i}-1}\frac{1-q^{y_{i}-\ell}}{1-q}\prod_{j=n}^{i+1}\left[\prod_{\ell=x_{j+1}+\theta}^{x_{j}}\frac{(1-q^{y_{i}-\ell})}{1-q}\right]\right)\\
=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{i+1}-\theta+1)\prod_{j=i+1}^{n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j})}\right)\\
=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{n+1})\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1})}.

(323)

Finally, we transform the last factor in the righthand side of (
321
):

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}q^{-\ell}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}q^{-y_{i}}\right)=q^{-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum\limits_{\ell=y_{i}+1}^{x_{i}}\ell+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{i-1}\sum\limits_{\ell=x_{j+1}+\theta}^{x_{j}}\ell\right)-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum\limits_{\ell=x_{i+1}+\theta}^{y_{i}-1}y_{i}+\sum\limits_{j=i+1}^{n}\sum\limits_{\ell=x_{j+1}+\theta}^{x_{j}}y_{i}\right)}\\
\propto q^{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}(y_{i}^{2}+y_{i})/2-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}(y_{i}-x_{n+1}-(n-i+1)\theta+n-i)}=q^{-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}^{2}/2+y_{i}(-x_{n+1}-(n-i+1)\theta+n-i-1/2)\right)}.

(324)

Combining (
320
), (
321
), (
322
) (
323
) and (
324
), we rewrite (
319
) as

\prod_{i<j}(1-q^{y_{i}-y_{j}})\prod_{i\leqslant j}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+1)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1})}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{q^{-\theta(n-i+1)y_{i}}q^{y_{i}^{2}/2-y_{i}(x_{n+1}+1/2)}w(y_{i})}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{1}-y_{i}+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{n+1})},

which matches (
65
) with
w(z)
as in (
66
).
∎

We recall the parameters
s^{2}=q^{v}
, and the coefficients (
73
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{{\bm{\lambda}}\setminus{\bm{\mu}}}^{(i)}(u;q,t,s)&=(u/t)^{|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|}t^{n({\bm{\mu}})-n({\bm{\lambda}})}\frac{C_{\bm{\lambda}}^{0}(s^{2}qt/u;q,t)C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(u/t;q,t)}{C_{\bm{\lambda}}^{0}(u;q,t)C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(s^{2}qt/u;q,t)}\\
&\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\lambda}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j-1}t^{-\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i+3}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}t^{\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i}}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\lambda}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}\neq\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}t^{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j}t^{-\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i+1}s^{2}}\\
&\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\mu}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j+1}t^{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}t^{2-\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i}s^{2}}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\mu}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}\neq\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}t^{2-\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j}t^{\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i+1}},\end{split}

(325)

\displaystyle C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(x;q,t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\mu}}}(1-q^{j-1}t^{1-i}x)=\prod_{i\geqslant 1}(t^{1-i}x;q)_{\mu_{i}}

We take
u=t^{n+1}
and
s=t^{n}\sqrt{t_{0}t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}/qt}
, and recall
t^{n}\sqrt{t_{0}t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}/qt}=q^{v/2}
. We have

\displaystyle(u/t)^{|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|}t^{n({\bm{\mu}})-n({\bm{\lambda}})}=t^{n(|{\bm{\lambda}}|-|{\bm{\mu}}|)+n({\bm{\mu}})-n({\bm{\lambda}})}=q^{-\theta(\sum_{i}(n-(i-1))y_{i}-\sum_{i}(n-(i-1))x_{i})}.

(326)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(u/t;q,t)}{C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(s^{2}qt/u;q,t)}=\frac{C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(t^{n};q,t)}{C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(q^{v+1}t^{-n};q,t)}=\frac{C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(q^{n\theta};q,t)}{C_{\bm{\mu}}^{0}(q^{v+1-n\theta};q,t)}\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\mu_{i}+(n-i+1)\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}((n-i+1)\theta)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(v+1-(n+i-1)\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(v+1-(n+i-1)\theta+\mu_{i})}\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+n\theta)\Gamma_{q}(v+1-(n+i-1)\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}((n-i+1)\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+v+1-n\theta)}.\end{split}

(327)

In the following, we simplify the products in
\psi_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}^{(i)}(u;q,t,s)
. For the first product term in (
325
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\lambda}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j-1}t^{-\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i+3}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}t^{\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i}}&=\prod_{k}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\lambda}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\mu_{j}^{\prime}=k}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j-1}t^{-k-i+3}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}t^{k-i}}\\
&=\prod_{k}\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k\atop\lambda_{k+1}+1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{k}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j-1}t^{-k-i+3}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}t^{k-i}}\\
&=\prod_{i\leqslant k}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+y_{k}+v+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-y_{k}+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+x_{k+1}+v+2\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{k+1}-\theta+1)}.\end{split}

(328)

For the second product term in (
325
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\lambda}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}\neq\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}t^{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j}t^{-\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i+1}s^{2}}&=\prod_{k}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\lambda}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=k,\mu_{j}^{\prime}=k-1}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}t^{k-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j}t^{-k-i+2}s^{2}}\\
&=\prod_{k}\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k\atop\mu_{k}+1\leqslant j\leqslant\lambda_{k}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}t^{k-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j}t^{-k-i+2}s^{2}}\\
&=\prod_{i\leqslant k}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{k}+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+y_{k}+1+v)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-x_{k}+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{k}+1+v)}.\end{split}

(329)

For the third product term in (
325
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\mu}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j+1}t^{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}t^{2-\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i}s^{2}}&=\prod_{k}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\mu}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=\mu_{j}^{\prime}=k}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j+1}t^{k-i}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}t^{2-k-i}s^{2}}\\
&=\prod_{k}\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k,\atop\lambda_{k+1}+1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{k}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j+1}t^{k-i}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}t^{2-k-i}s^{2}}\\
&=\prod_{i\leqslant k}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-x_{k+1}-\theta+1)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{k+1}+v+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{k}+1)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+y_{k}+v)}.\end{split}

(330)

Finally for the last product term in (
325
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\mu}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}\neq\mu_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}t^{2-\lambda_{j}^{\prime}-i}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j}t^{\mu_{j}^{\prime}-i+1}}&=\prod_{k}\prod_{(i,j)\in{\bm{\mu}}\atop\lambda_{j}^{\prime}=k+1,\mu_{j}^{\prime}=k}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}t^{1-k-i}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j}t^{k-i+1}}\\
&=\prod_{k}\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k\atop\mu_{k+1}+1\leqslant j\leqslant\lambda_{k+1}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}t^{1-k-i}s^{2}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j}t^{k-i+1}}\\
&=\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{k+1})\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+x_{k+1}+1+v)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-y_{k+1})\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+y_{k+1}+1+v)}.\end{split}

(331)

Putting (
326
), (
327
), (
328
), (
329
), (
330
), and (
331
) all together, we get the following expression for the transition probability (
74
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}({\bm{y}}|{\bm{x}})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[q^{-\theta(\sum_{i}(n-(i-1))y_{i}-\sum_{i}(n-(i-1))x_{i})}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+n\theta)\Gamma_{q}(v+1-(n+i-1)\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}((n-i+1)\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+v+1-n\theta)}\right]\\
&\prod_{i\leqslant k\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+y_{k}+v+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-y_{k}+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+x_{k+1}+2\theta+v)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{k+1}-\theta+1)}\prod_{i\leqslant k\leqslant n+1}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{k}+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+y_{k}+1+v)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-x_{k}+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{k}+1+v)}\\
&\prod_{i\leqslant k\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-x_{k+1}-\theta+1)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{k+1}+v+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{k}+1)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+y_{k}+v)}\prod_{i\leqslant k\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{k+1})\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+x_{k+1}+1+v)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-y_{k+1})\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}+y_{k+1}+1+v)}\\
&\propto\prod_{1\leqslant i<k\leqslant n}(1-q^{y_{i}-y_{k}})\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{k+1})\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{k}+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{k}+1)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{k+1}-\theta+1)}\\
&\times\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k\leqslant n}(1-q^{y_{i}+y_{k}+v})\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{k=1}^{n+1}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{k}+v+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{k}+1+v)}\right]\prod_{i=1}^{n}q^{-\theta\sum_{i=1}^{n}(n-(i-1))y_{i}}.\end{split}

(332)

This finishes the proof of (
75
). In the following we check that (
75
) is a special case of (
27
), with
b(z)=q^{-z}+q^{z+v}
. We notice that the difference factorizes:

\displaystyle b(z_{1})-b(z_{2})=q^{-z_{1}}+q^{z_{1}+v}-q^{-z_{2}}-q^{z_{2}+v}=(q^{-z_{1}}-q^{-z_{2}})(1-q^{z_{1}+z_{2}+v}).

We recall the transition probability (
27
)

\mathbb{P}({\bm{y}}|{\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{Z({\bm{x}})}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\bigl(b(y_{i})-b(y_{j})\bigr)\,\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[w(y_{i})\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1}{b(\ell)-b(y_{i})}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{1}{b(y_{i})-b(\ell)}\right].

(333)

With
b(z)=q^{-z}+q^{z+v}
, the first product becomes

\displaystyle\prod_{i<j}(b(y_{i})-b(y_{j}))=\prod_{i<j}(q^{-y_{i}}-q^{-y_{j}})(1-q^{y_{i}+y_{j}+v}).

(334)

For the denominators in (
333
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\bigl(b(\ell)-b(y_{i})\bigr)\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}\bigl(b(y_{i})-b(\ell)\bigr)\\
&=\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}(q^{-\ell}-q^{-y_{i}})(1-q^{y_{i}+\ell+v})\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}(q^{-y_{i}}-q^{-\ell})(1-q^{y_{i}+\ell+v})\\
&\propto\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell>y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}(q^{-\ell}-q^{-y_{i}})\prod\limits_{\begin{smallmatrix}\ell\in\mathbb{L}({\bm{x}})\\
\ell<y_{i}\end{smallmatrix}}(q^{-y_{i}}-q^{-\ell})\prod_{\ell\in\mathbb{L}\setminus\{y_{i}\}}(1-q^{\ell+y_{i}+v}).\end{split}

(335)

The first two products on the righthand side of (
335
) are computed in (
321
), (
322
), (
323
) and (
324
).
For the last product term on the righthand side of (
335
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\,\prod_{\ell\in\mathbb{L}\setminus\{y_{i}\}}(1-q^{\ell+y_{i}+v})&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\prod_{\ell\in\mathbb{L}}(1-q^{\ell+y_{i}+v})}{1-q^{2y_{i}+v}}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{n}\prod_{\ell=x_{j+1}+\theta}^{x_{j}}(1-q^{\ell+y_{i}+v})}{1-q^{2y_{i}+v}}\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[\frac{1}{1-q^{2y_{i}+v}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{j}+v+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{j+1}+v+\theta)}\right]\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-q^{2y_{i}+v}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{n+1}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{j}+v+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{j}+v+\theta)}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{1}+v+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{n+1}+v+1)}.\end{split}

(336)

Combining (
336
) with transformations of (
335
) from the previous proof, we rewrite (
333
) as

\displaystyle\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(1-q^{y_{i}-y_{j}})\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}(1-q^{y_{i}+y_{j}+v})\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{i}-y_{j}+1)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1})}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{j+1}-\theta+1)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{n+1}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{j}+v+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{j}+v+1)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[\frac{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{n+1}+v+1)}{\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}+x_{1}+v+\theta)}\frac{q^{-\sum_{i}(n-i+1)\theta y_{i}}q^{y_{i}^{2}/2-y_{i}(x_{n+1}+1/2)}w(y_{i})}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{1}-y_{i}+\theta)\Gamma_{q}(y_{i}-x_{n+1})}\right],

which matches (
332
) if

\displaystyle w(z)=q^{-z^{2}/2+z(x_{n+1}+1/2)}{\Gamma_{q}(x_{1}-z+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(z-x_{n+1})}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(z+x_{1}+v+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(z+x_{n+1}+v+1)}.

This finishes the proof of Claim
3.17
.
∎

Let us do some simplifications in
\psi^{(i)}_{\bm{\lambda}/\bm{\mu}}
of Theorem
3.16
in the
q=t
case. We additionally set
t^{2n}t_{0}t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}=p
.
Note that if
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}=\mu^{\prime}_{j}
, then
(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}
is equivalent to
(i,j)\in{{\bm{\mu}}}
. On the other hand, if
(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}/{{\bm{\mu}}}
, then necessarily
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}\neq\mu^{\prime}_{j}
. Hence,

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}\\
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}=\mu^{\prime}_{j}\end{subarray}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}q^{-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}p}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}q^{\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}\\
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}\neq\mu^{\prime}_{j}\end{subarray}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}q^{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}q^{-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i}p}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in{{\bm{\mu}}}\\
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}=\mu^{\prime}_{j}\end{subarray}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j+1}q^{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}q^{-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i}p}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in{{\bm{\mu}}}\\
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}\neq\mu^{\prime}_{j}\end{subarray}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}q^{-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}p}{1-q^{\mu_{i}-j}q^{\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+1}}\\
=\prod_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}}(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}q^{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i+1})(1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}q^{-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}p)\prod_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\mu}}}}\frac{1}{(1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}q^{\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i})(1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}q^{-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i}p)}\\
\times\prod_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}/{{\bm{\mu}}}}\frac{1}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}q^{-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i}p}\prod_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}/{{\bm{\mu}}}}\frac{1}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}q^{-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}p}.

We now simplify the last line. Note that since
\bm{\mu}\prec\bm{\lambda}
and
(i,j)\in\bm{\lambda}/\bm{\mu}
, we have
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}=i
and
\mu^{\prime}_{j}=i-1
. Hence, we get

\displaystyle\prod_{i}\left[\prod_{j=\mu_{i}+1}^{\lambda_{i}}\frac{1}{1-q^{\mu_{i}+j}q^{-2i}p}\right]\cdot\left[\prod_{j=\mu_{i}+1}^{\lambda_{i}}\frac{1}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}q^{-2i}p}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{i}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{2(\lambda_{i}-\mu_{i})}\frac{1}{1-q^{2\mu_{i}+j}q^{-2i}p}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{i}\frac{(q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+1}p;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{2(\mu_{i}-i)+1}p;q)_{\infty}}.

Let us now rewrite the branching rule for Koornwinder polynomials at
q=t
:

Lemma A.1
.

Consider Koornwinder polynomials with
t=q
,
t_{0}=r\cdot t^{-n-1}
. Define the quasi-branching coefficient through:

K^{(n+1)}_{\bm{\lambda}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n},r\cdot q^{-n-1};\,q,q;\,r\cdot q^{-n-1},t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\\
=\sum_{\bm{\mu}\prec\bm{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}\cdot K^{(n)}_{\bm{\mu}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n};\,q,q;\,r\cdot q^{-n},t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}).

Then we have:

\tilde{\psi}_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}=\frac{g_{n+1}({\bm{\lambda}})}{g_{n}({\bm{\mu}})}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\left[q^{-\lambda_{i}}\frac{1-q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}{1-q^{-2i+n}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\right].

where
g_{n}({{\bm{\mu}}})
is

(r)^{-|{{\bm{\mu}}}|}C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}/q^{2})C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{1}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{2}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{3}/q)\cdot q^{(n+1)|{{\bm{\mu}}}|}\prod_{i\geqslant 1}\frac{(q^{2(\mu_{i}-i)+n}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{-2i+n}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3};q)_{\infty}}.

(337)

We denote
q^{2n}t_{0}t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}=p
. Comparing with Theorem
3.16
, we have

\tilde{\psi}_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}=\psi^{(i)}_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}\left(q^{n+1};\,q,q,q^{-1}\sqrt{p}\right)\cdot\frac{k^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(q,q,q^{n+1};\,r\cdot q^{-n-1}:t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})}{k^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(q,q,q^{n};\,r\cdot q^{-n}:t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})}.

Using the above simplifications for
\psi^{(i)}_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}
, the last expression is

\frac{q^{n|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|-n({{\bm{\lambda}}})}}{q^{n|{{\bm{\mu}}}|-n({{\bm{\mu}}})}}\frac{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(pq^{-1-n})C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(q^{n})}{C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(pq^{-1-n})C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(q^{n+1})}\frac{\prod\limits_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}}(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}q^{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i+1})(1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}q^{-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}p)}{\prod\limits_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\mu}}}}(1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}q^{\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i})(1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}q^{-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i}p)}\prod_{i}\frac{(q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+1}p;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{2(\mu_{i}-i)+1}p;q)_{\infty}}\\
\times\frac{(r/q)^{-|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}q^{n({{\bm{\lambda}}})}}{(r/q)^{-|{{\bm{\mu}}}|}q^{n({{\bm{\mu}}})}}\frac{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(q^{n+1})C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{1}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{2}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{3}/q)}{C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(q^{n})C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{1}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{2}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{3}/q)}\frac{\prod\limits_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\mu}}}}(1-q^{\mu_{i}-j+1}q^{\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i})(1-q^{\mu_{i}+j-1}q^{-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i}p)}{\prod\limits_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}}(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}q^{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i+1})(1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j}q^{-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}p)}\\
=\frac{q^{(n+1)|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}}{q^{(n+1)|{{\bm{\mu}}}|}}\frac{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(pq^{-1-n})}{C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(pq^{-1-n})}\frac{(r)^{-|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}}{(r)^{-|{{\bm{\mu}}}|}}\frac{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{1}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{2}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{3}/q)}{C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{1}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{2}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rt_{3}/q)}\prod_{i}\frac{(q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+1}p;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{2(\mu_{i}-i)+1}p;q)_{\infty}},

with all
C^{0}
being evaluated at
(\cdot;\,q,q)
. It remains to note that

p=q^{n-1}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}.\qed

Here is a restatement of Lemma
A.1
: using the notation (
337
) we define a symmetric function
\tilde{K}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})
through

\tilde{K}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n}):=\frac{K^{(n)}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n};\,q,q;\,r\cdot q^{-n},t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})}{g_{n}({{\bm{\mu}}})}.

(It still depends on
q,r,t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}
, but we are hiding this dependence.) Then we have

\tilde{K}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n},rq^{-n-1})=\sum_{{{\bm{\mu}}}\prec{{\bm{\lambda}}}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\left[q^{-\lambda_{i}}\frac{1-q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}{1-q^{-2i+n}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\right]\right]\tilde{K}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n}).

Let us now match to the notations of Theorem
3.19
. For that we transform the weight arising in branching of
n
(rather than
n+1
) variable polynomial

q^{-\lambda_{i}}\frac{1-q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n-1}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}{1-q^{-2i+n-1}rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}=\frac{\sqrt{rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\,q^{\lambda_{i}+\frac{n-1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\,q^{\lambda_{i}+(n-1)/2-i}}}{\sqrt{rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\,q^{(n-1)/2-i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\,q^{(n-1)/2-i}}}.

(338)

Denoting
x_{i}=\lambda_{i}+\frac{n+1}{2}-i
and
\sigma=q^{-1}\sqrt{rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}
and removing the
\lambda_{i}
–independent normalization, we get the weight

\sigma q^{x_{i}}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{x_{i}}}.

We are now ready to finish the proof of Theorem
3.19
. We compute

\sum_{\emptyset={{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(0)}\prec{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(1)}\prec\dots\prec{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(n)}={{\bm{\lambda}}}}\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(\sigma q^{\lambda^{(k)}_{i}+\frac{k+1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{\lambda^{(k)}_{i}+\frac{k+1}{2}-i}}\right),

(339)

which differs from the left-hand side of (
77
) by allowing
k=n
.
Using Lemma
A.1
recurrently for
n-1
,
n-2
,…, adopting the notation
\sigma=q^{-1}\sqrt{rt_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}
, and transforming the weights as in (
338
), the sum (
339
) evaluates to

\left[\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}}\right)\right]\frac{K^{(n)}_{{\bm{\mu}}}(rq^{-1},rq^{-2},\dots,rq^{-n};\,q,q;\,r\cdot q^{-n},t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})}{g_{n}({{\bm{\lambda}}})}.

(340)

For the principal specialization of Koornwinder polynomials we use another formula from
[
98
]
:

K^{(n+1)}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(t_{0},t_{0}t,\dots,t_{0}t^{n};\,q,t;\,t_{0},t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})=(t_{0}t^{n})^{-|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}t^{n({{\bm{\lambda}}})}\frac{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(t^{n+1},t^{n}t_{0}t_{1},t^{n}t_{0}t_{2},t^{n}t_{0}t_{3};\,q,t)}{C_{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{-}(t;\,q,t)C_{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{+}(t^{2n}t_{0}t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}/q;\,q,t)},

where
C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}
is as in Theorem
3.16
and

\displaystyle C^{+}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(x;q,t)

\displaystyle:=\prod_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}}(1-q^{\lambda_{i}+j-1}t^{2-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}x)=\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant l}\frac{(q^{\lambda_{i}}t^{2-l-i}x;q)}{(q^{2\lambda_{i}}t^{2-2i}x;q)}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant l}\frac{(q^{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}t^{3-i-j}x;q)}{(q^{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}t^{2-i-j}x;q)},

\displaystyle C^{-}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(x;q,t)

\displaystyle:=\prod_{(i,j)\in{{\bm{\lambda}}}}(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-j}t^{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}-i}x)=\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant l}\frac{(x;q)}{(q^{\lambda_{i}}t^{l-i}x;q)}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant l}\frac{(q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i}x;q)}{(q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i-1}x;q)}.

Hence, (
340
) becomes

\left[\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}}\right)\right](r/q)^{-|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}q^{n({{\bm{\lambda}}})}\frac{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(q^{n})C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{1}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{2}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{3}/q)}{C_{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{-}(q;\,q,q)C_{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{+}(\sigma q^{n-1};\,q,q)}\\
\times(r)^{|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}\frac{1}{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(\sigma^{2})C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{1}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{2}/q)C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(rt_{3}/q)\cdot q^{(n+1)|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}}\prod_{i\geqslant 1}\frac{(q^{-2i+n+2}\sigma^{2};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+2}\sigma^{2};q)_{\infty}}\\
=\left[\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}}\right)\right]q^{-n|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}q^{n({{\bm{\lambda}}})}\frac{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(q^{n})}{C_{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{-}(q;\,q,q)C_{{\bm{\lambda}}}^{+}(\sigma^{2}q^{n-1};\,q,q)}\\
\times\frac{1}{C^{0}_{{\bm{\lambda}}}(\sigma^{2})}\cdot\prod_{i\geqslant 1}\frac{(q^{-2i+n+1}\sigma^{2};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+1}\sigma^{2};q)_{\infty}}\\
=\left[\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}}\right)\right]q^{-n|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|+n({{\bm{\lambda}}})}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\bigl(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}q^{j-i}\bigr)\bigl(1-\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}+n-i-j+1}\bigr)\\
\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[\frac{(q^{\lambda_{i}+n-i+1};q)_{\infty}}{(q;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(\sigma^{2}q^{-2i+n+2};q)_{\infty}}{(\sigma^{2}q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+2};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(\sigma^{2}q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+1};q)_{\infty}}{(\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{i}-i+1};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(q^{1-i}q^{n};q)_{\lambda_{i}}}{(\sigma^{2}q^{1-i};q)_{\lambda_{i}}}\right].

It remains to transform the last formula.
First, let us simplify the factor in
\prod_{i=1}^{n}
:

\frac{(q^{\lambda_{i}+n-i+1};q)_{\infty}}{(q;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(\sigma^{2}q^{-2i+n+2};q)_{\infty}}{(\sigma^{2}q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+2};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(\sigma^{2}q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+1};q)_{\infty}}{(\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{i}-i+1};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(q^{1-i}q^{n};q)_{\lambda_{i}}}{(\sigma^{2}q^{1-i};q)_{\lambda_{i}}}\\
=\frac{(q^{n-i+1};q)_{\infty}}{(q;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(\sigma^{2}q^{-2i+n+2};q)_{\infty}}{(\sigma^{2}q^{1-i};q)_{\infty}}\bigl(1-\sigma^{2}q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+1}\bigr)=\frac{1-\sigma^{2}q^{2(\lambda_{i}-i)+n+1}}{(q;q)_{n-i}\,(\sigma^{2}q^{1-i};q)_{n-i+1}}

(341)

Next, let us modify the
\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{k}
factor:

\left[\prod_{k=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{\frac{k+1}{2}-i}}\right)\right]=(-\sigma)^{-n(n+1)/2}\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k\leqslant n}\left(1-\sigma^{2}q^{k+1-2i}\right)\\
=(-\sigma)^{-n(n+1)/2}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\sigma^{2}q^{1-i};q)_{n-i+1}.

(342)

We observe a nice cancellation between
q
-Pochammer symbols involving
\sigma
in the last two formulas. Finally, we rewrite the remaining factors:

q^{-n|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|+n({{\bm{\lambda}}})}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\bigl(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}q^{j-i}\bigr)\bigl(1-\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}+n-i-j+1}\bigr)\\
=q^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(i-1-n)\lambda_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\bigl(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+j-i}-\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}+n-i-j+1}+\sigma^{2}q^{2\lambda_{i}+n-2i+1}\bigr)\\
=q^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(i-1-n)\lambda_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}q^{\lambda_{i}-i+\frac{n+1}{2}}\left(q^{i-\lambda_{i}-\frac{n+1}{2}}+\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{i}-i+\frac{n+1}{2}}-q^{j-\lambda_{j}-\frac{n+1}{2}}-\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{j}-j+\frac{n+1}{2}}\right)\\
=\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}q^{(\frac{n+1}{2}-i)(n-i)}\right]q^{-|{{\bm{\lambda}}}|}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left(q^{i-\lambda_{i}-\frac{n+1}{2}}+\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{i}-i+\frac{n+1}{2}}-q^{j-\lambda_{j}-\frac{n+1}{2}}-\sigma^{2}q^{\lambda_{j}-j+\frac{n+1}{2}}\right)

(343)

It remains to multiply (
341
), (
342
), (
343
) and divide by
\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k}\left(\sigma q^{\lambda_{i}+\frac{k+1}{2}-i}-\frac{1}{\sigma q^{\lambda_{i}+\frac{k+1}{2}-i}}\right)
to get the result.
∎
